\mathbb{V}\left[n_{1}(G)\right]=(1+o(1))ce^{-c}n\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathbb{V}\left[n_{0}(G)\right]=o(\mathbb{V}\left[n_{1}(G)\right]).

Hence, it remains to prove that
\mathbb{V}\left[Y\right]=o(ce^{-c}n)=o(\mathbb{V}\left[n_{1}(G)\right])
. Now, by considering
Y
as a sum of weighted components we have

Y=\sum_{C}(\Phi(C)-n_{0}(C)-n_{1}(C)),

where the sum is taken over components
C
of
G[P\cup R]
.
However, we note that the contribution to this sum from components
C
containing at most one red vertex is
0
. Indeed, by
Observation

4.1
, all components contain at least one red vertex, and for a component
C
containing a unique red vertex
w
it easy to verify that
\Phi(C)=1
if
d(w)\leq 1
and
\Phi(C)=0
otherwise. Hence, since each vertex of degree at most
1
is always red, in each case it follows that
\Phi(C)-n_{0}(C)-n_{1}(C)=0
.
Thus we can rewrite
Y
as

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\\
|C\cap R|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\Phi(C)-n_{0}(C)-n_{1}(C)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\\
|C\cap R|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\sum_{v\in C}\frac{\Phi(C)-n_{0}(C)-n_{1}(C)}{|C|}.

For every component
C
of
G[P\cup R]
and
w\in C
, let
\psi^{\prime}_{G}(w)=\frac{\Phi(C)-n_{0}(C)-n_{1}(C)}{|C|}
and let
\psi^{\prime}_{G}(w)=0
for
w\in S(G)
.
If we let
D^{\prime}\coloneqq\{w\in V(G)\colon\psi^{\prime}_{G^{+}}(w)\neq\psi^{\prime}_{G^{-}}(w)\}
, then
by
Lemma

3.6

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left[Y\right]\leq 2p(1-p)n^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|D^{\prime}|^{2}\right]\leq 2cn\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[|D^{\prime}|^{2}\right].

Note that
Observation

4.7
also holds with
\phi
replaced by
\psi^{\prime}
. Hence similar to
Lemma

4.12
, we get that
D^{\prime}\subseteq W^{*}\coloneqq W^{-}\cup W^{+}\cup\{u,v\}
. Suppose that
D^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
and let
w\in D^{\prime}
.
Then
\psi^{\prime}_{G^{+}}(w)\neq\psi^{\prime}_{G^{-}}(w)
, hence
\psi^{\prime}_{G^{+}}(w)\neq 0
or
\psi^{\prime}_{G^{-}}(w)\neq 0
.
Assume that
\psi^{\prime}_{G^{+}}(w)\neq 0
(the proof proceeds analogously in the other case).
It follows that
w\in(R^{+}\cup P^{+})\cap W^{*}\subseteq W^{-}\cup W^{+}
and that
C_{w}^{+}
is a red-purple component in
G^{+}
with at least
2
red vertices (as otherwise
\psi^{\prime}(w)=0
).
By
Lemma

4.9
, we have
N_{G^{+}}(W^{-}\cup W^{+})\subseteq S^{+}
and thus
C_{w}^{+}\subseteq W^{-}\cup W^{+}\subseteq W^{*}
(since
C_{w}^{+}
is a component of
G^{+}-S^{+}
).
Since
W^{*}_{R}\eqqcolon W^{*}\cap(R^{-}\cup R^{+})\supseteq W^{+}\cap R^{+}\supseteq C_{w}^{+}\cap R^{+}
, we have
|W^{*}_{R}|\geq|C_{w}^{+}\cap R^{+}|\geq 2
.
Hence, by
Lemma

4.14
we have that if
D^{\prime}\neq\varnothing
then
G^{+}
spans a tree
T
containing the vertices
u
and
v
, at least
\ell\geq 2
vertices with no neighbour outside
T
, and at most
4\ell+2
vertices in total.

Similarly to (
18
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|D^{\prime}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=2}^{4\log^{4}n}\sum_{j=2}^{4\ell+2}j^{2}\binom{n-2}{j-2}j^{j-2}p^{j-2}\binom{j}{\ell}(1-p)^{\ell(n-j)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=2}^{4\log^{4}n}\sum_{j=2}^{4\ell+2}j^{2}\left(\frac{e(n-2)jp}{\max\{j-2,1\}}\right)^{j-2}2^{j}e^{-p\ell(n-j)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=2}^{4\log^{4}n}\sum_{j=2}^{4\ell+2}4j^{2}(20c)^{j-2}e^{-(1+o(1))c\ell}=c^{8}e^{-(1+o(1))2c},

where at the last equality we used that
c\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left[Y\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2p(1-p)n^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|D|^{2}\right]\leq 2nc^{9}e^{-(1+o(1))2c}=o(ce^{-c}n).

Appendix B
Proof of Lemma
3.8

By
Theorem

3.7
, it suffices to show that
\delta\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
(where
\delta
is defined as in
11
).
Note that for any
U\in\mathcal{U}
,

(58)

\displaystyle\mu_{U}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U}\right]=O(p^{|U|-1}),

where we used the fact that
|U|\leq t=O(1)
.

We claim that for all
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
with
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing
and
U_{1}\cap U_{3}=\varnothing
, the following inequalities hold.

(59)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq Y_{U_{1}}\leq Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}\leq Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}\cup U_{3}}\leq T_{U_{1}},

(60)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{1}}\leq Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})=\varnothing\}}\leq T_{U_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})=\varnothing\}},\text{ and }

(61)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}\cup U_{3}}-Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}\leq Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}\cup U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{3})=\varnothing\}}\leq T_{U_{1}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})=\varnothing\}}.

All these inequalities except the middle inequalities in
60
and
61
follow directly from
12
. Note that if
E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})=\varnothing
, then, by the definition of
Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}
and the fact that
Y_{U_{1}}
is semi-induced with respect to
U_{1}
, we have
Y_{U_{1}}=Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}
. Hence
Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{1}}\leq Y_{U_{1}}^{U_{2}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})=\varnothing\}}
. The middle inequality in
61
follows analogously.

For
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}
with
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing
, if both
G[U_{1}]
and
G[U_{2}]
are connected then so is
G[U_{1}\cup U_{2}]
. Thus,

(62)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathds{1}_{\{G[U_{1}\cup U_{2}]\text{ is connected}\}}\right]=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}|-1}).

Similarly, for
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
with
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing
and
(U_{1}\cup U_{2})\cap U_{3}\neq\varnothing
, if each of
G[U_{1}]
,
G[U_{2}]
, and
G[U_{3}]
is connected then so is
G[U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}]
. Thus

(63)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]&=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).\end{split}

We deal with the three terms in
11
separately.

Claim B.1
.

We have
\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U}|Z_{U}^{2}\right]=o(1)
.

Proof of Claim.

Note that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sigma_{Y}^{2}Z_{U_{1}}^{2}&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}2Y_{U_{2}}(Y_{U_{3}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}})\\
&\quad\quad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\end{split}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}),

since
Y_{U_{3}}\leq Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}
.
Together with
|X_{U}|\leq\sigma_{Y}^{-1}(Y_{U}+\mu_{U})
, this implies that

(64)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sigma_{Y}^{3}&\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U}|Z_{U}^{2}\right]\leq\\
&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}\right]\right)\\
+\quad\quad\quad&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]\right).\end{split}

Let
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
with
U_{2}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing
and
U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing
.
If
U_{2}\cap U_{3}=\varnothing
, then

\displaystyle\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}|-1+|U_{2}|-1+|U_{3}|-1})

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}|-1+|U_{2}|-|U_{1}\cap U_{2}|+|U_{3}|-|(U_{1}\cup U_{2})\cap U_{3}|})=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

If
U_{2}\cap U_{3}\neq\varnothing
, then, by
62
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}|-1+|U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}|-|U_{1}\cap(U_{2}\cup U_{3})|+|U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

Hence, using
63
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

It follows that

(65)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}\right]\right)\\
=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing,\,U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})=O(n).\end{split}

Now let
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
with
U_{2}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing
and
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing
.
If
U_{2}\cap U_{3}=\varnothing
, then, by
59
,
60
,
58
, and independence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=2\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{3}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\right]

\displaystyle\quad=O(p^{|U_{1}|+|U_{2}|+|U_{3}|-1})=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}),

since
\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\right],\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\right]=O(p)
.
If
U_{2}\cap U_{3}\neq\varnothing
, then, by
59
,
60
,
58
,
62
, and independence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=O(p^{|U_{1}|+|U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

It follows that

(66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[(Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{2}})(Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}})\right]\right)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{2}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})=O(n).\end{split}

Hence, by
64
, we have

\sigma_{Y}^{3}\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U}|Z_{U}^{2}\right]=O(n).

Since,
\sigma_{Y}=\omega(n^{\frac{1}{3}})
, we have
\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U}|Z_{U}^{2}\right]=o(1)
.
∎

Next we bound the second term in
11
.

Claim B.2
.

We have
\sum_{U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U_{1}}Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}V_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]=o(1)
.

Proof of Claim.

Note that, by definition, if
U_{1}=U_{2}
, then
V_{U_{1}U_{2}}=0
. Hence, we may sum only over
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}
with
U_{1}\neq U_{2}
. By substituting the definitions of
Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}
and
V_{U_{1}U_{2}}
, using
|X_{U_{1}}|\leq\sigma_{Y}^{-1}(Y_{U_{1}}+\mu_{U_{1}})
, and applying the triangle inequality, we have

(67)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sigma_{Y}^{3}\quad&\sum_{U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U_{1}}Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}V_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]\leq\\
&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing,U_{1}\neq U_{2}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing,U_{3}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]\right)\\
+\quad\quad&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing,U_{1}\neq U_{2}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing,U_{3}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]\right)\\
+\quad\quad&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing,U_{3}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]\right)\\
+\quad\quad&\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing,U_{3}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}||Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}||Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]\right).\end{split}

Since,
\sigma_{Y}=\omega(n^{\frac{1}{3}})
, it suffices to show that each of the four sums in
67
is
O(n)
.

For the first sum in
67
, let
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
such that
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing
,
U_{1}\neq U_{2}
,
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing
, and
U_{3}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing
.
By
59
,
58
,
62
, and
63
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})+O(p^{|U_{1}|-1}p^{|U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})+O(p^{|U_{1}|-1+|U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-|U_{1}\cap(U_{2}\cup U_{3})|})

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

Hence, the first sum in
67
is
O(n)
.

For the second sum, let
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
such that
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing
,
U_{1}\neq U_{2}
,
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing
, and
U_{3}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing
. By
59
,
61
,
58
,
62
, and independence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{2},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{2},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{3}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{2},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{3}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{2},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\right]

\displaystyle\quad=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}|-1+|U_{3}|})+O(p^{|U_{1}|+|U_{2}|+|U_{3}|-2})

\displaystyle\quad=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

Hence, the second sum is
O(n)
.

For the third sum, let
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
such that
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing
,
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing
, and
U_{3}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing
.
By
59
,
60
,
58
,
62
, and independence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}T_{U_{3}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}T_{U_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=2\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad=O(p^{|U_{1}|+|U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1})=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

Hence, the third sum is
O(n)
.

For the fourth sum, let
U_{1},U_{2},U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}
such that
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing
,
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing
, and
U_{3}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing
. By
59
,
60
,
61
,
58
, and independence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}||Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}||Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}\cup U_{2}}|\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{2},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{2},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{3}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{2},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\right]

\displaystyle\quad=O(p^{|U_{1}|+|U_{2}|+|U_{3}|-1})=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}\cup U_{3}|-1}).

Hence, the fourth sum is
O(n)
. This completes the proof of the claim.
∎

Finally we bound the third term in
11
.

Claim B.3
.

We have
\sum_{U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U_{1}}Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{U_{1}}+V_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]=o(1)
.

Proof of Claim.

We begin by showing that for any
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{U_{1}}|\right]=O(\sigma_{Y}^{-1})
and
\mathbb{E}\left[|V_{U_{1},U_{2}}|\right]=O(\sigma_{Y}^{-1})
.
By the triangle inequality,
60
,
58
, and independence we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{Y}\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{U_{1}}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{3}}\right]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{U_{3}}-Y_{U_{3}}^{U_{1}}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}\neq\varnothing\end{subarray}}O(p^{|U_{3}|-1})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle=O(1)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{3}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{3})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle=O(1)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{3}\in\mathcal{U}\\
U_{3}\cap U_{1}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}O(p^{|U_{3}|})=O(1).

Hence,
\mathbb{E}\left[|Z_{U_{1}}|\right]=O(\sigma_{Y}^{-1})
. Similarly,
\mathbb{E}\left[|V_{U_{1},U_{2}}|\right]=O(\sigma_{Y}^{-1})
.

Since,
\sigma_{Y}=\omega(n^{\frac{1}{3}})
, it now suffices to show that

(68)

\displaystyle\sigma_{Y}^{2}\sum_{U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U_{1}}Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]=O(n).

Let
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}
.
If
U_{1}=U_{2}
, then we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{Y}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U_{1}}Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}^{2}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}^{2}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq K^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathds{1}_{\{G[U_{1}]\text{ is a tree}\}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}^{2}=O(p^{|U_{1}|-1}).

Hence, the contribution of terms of this type to the sum in
68
is
O(n)
.
If
U_{1}\cap U_{2}\neq\varnothing
and
U_{1}\neq U_{2}
, then by
59
and
62
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{Y}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U_{1}}Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mu_{U_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mu_{U_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}|-1})+O(p^{|U_{1}|+|U_{2}|-2})

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}|-1}).

Hence, the contribution of terms of this type to the sum in
68
is
O(n)
.
If
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing
, then by
59
,
60

58
and independence, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{Y}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|X_{U_{1}}Z_{U_{1}U_{2}}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}|\right]+\mu_{U_{1}}\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{U_{2}}-Y_{U_{2}}^{U_{1}}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}T_{U_{2}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}\mathds{1}_{\{E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\}}\right]

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[T_{U_{2}}\right]\mathbb{P}\left[E_{G}(U_{1},U_{2})\neq\varnothing\right]

\displaystyle=O(p^{|U_{1}|+|U_{2}|-1})=O(p^{|U_{1}\cup U_{2}|-1}).

Hence, the contribution of terms of this type to the sum in
68
is
O(n)
. This completes the proof of the claim.
∎

By
Claim

B.1
,
Claim

B.2
, and
Claim

B.3
, we have
\delta\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
(where
\delta
is defined as in
11
). Hence, by
Theorem

3.7
, it follows that

\frac{Y-\mathbb{E}\left[Y\right]}{\sigma_{Y}}\quad\overset{d}{\to}\quad\mathcal{N}(0,1)\quad\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

as desired.

Appendix C
Proof of Lemma
4.15

For
\circ\in\{-,+\}
, let
\mathcal{E}^{\circ}
be the event that
G^{\circ}-S^{\circ}
contains a component of size larger than
\log^{4}n
.
Note that
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{comp}}^{c}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}^{+}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}^{-}\right]
. We show that
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}^{+}\right]=o(n^{-2})
. The proof that
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}^{-}\right]=o(n^{-2})
is analogous. Note that we may assume that
n
is large enough.

Let
R_{t}^{+}
and
P_{t}^{+}
be the sets of red and purple vertices after
t
iterations of the while-loop in
Subsection

2.1
applied to
G^{+}
, respectively. Moreover, let
m_{t}^{+}
be the size of the largest component in
G^{+}[R_{t}^{+}\cup P_{t}^{+}]
.

Every component of
G^{+}[R_{t+1}^{+}\cup P_{t+1}^{+}]
consists of a union of components of
G^{+}[R_{t}^{+}\cup P_{t}^{+}]
or vertices in
S_{t}^{+}
, that are connected by edges incident to the vertices that were recoloured in the
(t+1)
-th iteration. Since in every iteration a single vertex is recoloured red and at most
3
of its neighbours are recoloured purple, it follows that
m_{t+1}^{+}\leq 4\Delta(G^{+})\max(m_{t}^{+},1)
.

Suppose that
G^{+}-S^{+}=G^{+}[R^{+}\cup P^{+}]
contains a component of size larger than
\log^{4}n
. Then at least one of the following holds.

(i)

\Delta(G^{+})\geq\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}n
,

(ii)

There exists
t\geq 1
such that
\log^{2}n\leq m_{t}^{+}\leq\log^{4}n
.

It follows that

(69)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}^{+}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\Delta(G^{+})\geq\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}n\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\exists t\geq 1\colon\log^{2}n\leq m_{t}^{+}\leq\log^{4}n\right].

It now suffices to show that both of these terms are
o(n^{-2})
.
We bound the first term in (
69
) as follows.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\Delta(G^{+})\geq\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}n\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\Delta(G)\geq\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}n-1\right]\leq n\mathbb{P}\left[\text{Bin}\left(n-1,\frac{c}{n}\right)\geq\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}n-1\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}n+1\right)=o(n^{-2}),

where the last inequality follows from
Theorem

3.3
.

To bound the second term in (
69
) we use the following facts. For any component
C
of
G^{+}[R_{t}^{+}\cup P_{t}^{+}]
, the graph
G^{+}[C]
is connected and by
Observation

4.1
,
|R_{t}^{+}\cap C|\geq\frac{|C|}{4}
.
Moreover, since in
G^{+}
there are no edges from
R_{t}^{+}
to
V(G)\setminus(R_{t}^{+}\cup P_{t}^{+})
, there are no edges in
G^{+}
from
C\cap R_{t}^{+}
to
V(G)\setminus C
.

If particular, given a subset
C\subseteq V(G)
of size
i\coloneqq|C|=o(n)
, if
C
is a component of
G^{+}
, then
G
must contain all but one edge of a spanning tree of
C
and no edges from
C\cap R_{t}^{+}
to
V(G)\setminus C
and we can calculate

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[C\text{ is a component of $G^{+}[R_{t}^{+}\cup P_{t}^{+}]$ for some $t\geq 1$}\right]

\displaystyle\leq i^{i-2}p^{i-2}\binom{i}{\lceil\frac{i}{4}\rceil}(1-p)^{\frac{i(n-i)}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(2ip\exp\left(-\frac{c}{4}\right)\right)^{i-2},

using the fact that
\binom{i}{\lceil\frac{i}{4}\rceil}\leq 2^{i}
,
1-p\leq e^{-p}
and
i=o(n)
.

It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\exists t\geq 1\colon\log^{2}n\leq m_{t}^{+}\leq\log^{4}n\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\log^{2}n\leq i\leq\log^{4}n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}C\subseteq V(G)\\
|C|=i\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\left[C\text{ is a component of $G^{+}[R_{t}^{+}\cup P_{t}^{+}]$ for some $t\geq 1$}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\log^{2}n\leq i\leq\log^{4}n}4\binom{n}{i}\left(2ip\exp\left(-\frac{c}{4}\right)\right)^{i-2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\log^{2}n\leq i\leq\log^{4}n}\left(\frac{2en}{i}\right)^{2}\left(2ec\exp\left(-\frac{c}{4}\right)\right)^{i-2}

\displaystyle\quad=o(n^{-2}),

bounding the second term in
69
and completing the proof.

Appendix D
Proof of Lemma
5.3

In order to prove
Lemma

5.3
, we need the following bound on the probability that the
k
-th neighbourhood-ball is large.

Proposition D.1
.

Let
c>0
and let
G\sim G\left(n,\frac{c}{n}\right)
. Then, for each vertex
w\in V(G)
and
s>(7c)^{k}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|\geq s\right]\leq k\exp\left(-s^{\frac{1}{k}}\right).

Proof.

We can think of exposing
B_{G}(w,k)
by running a breadth-first-search algorithm in
G
rooted at
w
to depth
k
. A standard argument shows that we can couple the tree formed in this way from below by a Galton–Watson tree with child distribution
\mathrm{Bin}\left(n,\frac{c}{n}\right)
. Let
Z_{0},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{k}
denote the sizes of the
k+1
layers of the Galton–Watson tree (in particular
Z_{0}=1
). We see that by
Theorem

3.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|\geq s\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[Z_{k}\geq s\right]\leq\sum_{i\in[k]}\mathbb{P}\left[Z_{i}\geq s^{\frac{1}{k}}Z_{i-1}\;\middle|\;Z_{i-1}\geq 1\right]\leq k\exp\left(-s^{\frac{1}{k}}\right),

since conditioned on the value of
Z_{i-1}
, we have that
Z_{i}
has distribution
\mathrm{Bin}\left(nZ_{i-1},\frac{c}{n}\right)
.
∎

In the resulting calculations we will also need the following proposition.

Proposition D.2
.

Let
a\geq 1
and
m_{1},m_{2},s_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
with
s_{0}\geq\max\{a(2(m_{1}+1)(m_{2}+1))^{2m_{2}},a^{m_{2}+1}\}
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq s_{0}+1}s^{m_{1}}\exp\left(-\left(\frac{s}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{s_{0}}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right).

Proof.

Fix
a\geq 1
,
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and
s_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
with
s_{0}\geq\max\{a(2(m_{1}+1)(m_{2}+1))^{2m_{2}},a^{m_{2}+1}\}
.
Consider the function
g(x)\coloneqq x^{m_{1}}\exp\left(-\left(\frac{x}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right)
. Since

g^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{m_{2}}x^{m_{1}-1}\exp\left(-\left(\frac{x}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right)(m_{1}m_{2}-\left(\frac{x}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}),

g(x)
is decreasing for
x\geq a(m_{1}m_{2})^{m_{2}}
. Since
a(m_{1}m_{2})^{m_{2}}\leq a(2(m_{1}+1)(m_{2}+1))^{2m_{2}}\leq s_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq s_{0}+1}s^{m_{1}}\exp\left(-\left(\frac{s}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right)\leq\int_{s_{0}}^{\infty}x^{m_{1}}\exp\left(-\left(\frac{x}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right)dx.

Substituting
u=\left(\frac{x}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}
gives

\displaystyle\int_{s_{0}}^{\infty}x^{m_{1}}\exp\left(-\left(\frac{x}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right)dx=a^{m_{1}+1}m_{2}\int_{\left(\frac{s_{0}}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}}^{\infty}u^{(m_{1}+1)m_{2}-1}e^{-u}du.

Note that
u\geq\left(\frac{s_{0}}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\geq\max\{(2(m_{1}+1)(m_{2}+1))^{2},a\}
. In particular,
\log a\leq\log u
,
\log(2m_{2})\leq\log u
,
and
(m_{1}+1)(m_{2}+1)\sqrt{u}\leq\frac{u}{2}
.
Hence

\displaystyle(m_{1}+1)\log a+\log(2m_{2})+((m_{1}+1)m_{2}-1)\log u

\displaystyle\leq(m_{1}+1)(m_{2}+1)\log u

\displaystyle\leq(m_{1}+1)(m_{2}+1)\sqrt{u}\leq\frac{u}{2}.

Thus,
a^{m_{1}+1}m_{2}u^{(m_{1}+1)m_{2}-1}\leq\frac{1}{2}e^{\frac{u}{2}}
. It follows that

\displaystyle a^{m_{1}+1}m_{2}\int_{(s_{0}/a)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}}^{\infty}u^{(m_{1}+1)m_{2}-1}e^{-u}du\leq a^{m_{1}+1}m_{2}\int_{(s_{0}/a)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2}e^{-\frac{u}{2}}du=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{s_{0}}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{m_{2}}}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove
Lemma

5.3
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.3
.

Note that, by symmetry,

\mathbb{P}\left[|N^{*}|=s\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[|N^{*}|\geq s\right]\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(u,k)|\geq\frac{s}{2}\right]\leq 2k\exp\left(-\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{k}}\right),

where the last inequality follows from
Proposition

D.1
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|N^{*}|^{2}\mathds{1}_{\{|N^{*}|>t_{k}\}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=t_{k}+1}^{n}s^{2}\mathbb{P}\left[|N^{*}|=s\right]\leq\sum_{s=t_{k}+1}^{n}2s^{2}k\exp\left(-\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{k}}\right)

(70)

\displaystyle\leq 2k\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right),

where the last inequality follows by
Proposition

D.2
with
a=2,m_{1}=2
and
m_{2}=k
, since
t_{k}\coloneqq(10ck)^{2k}\geq 2(6(k+1))^{2k}
. This establishes
(i)
.

Fix a vertex
w\in V(G)\setminus\{u,v\}
and note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|N^{*}\cap(U_{N}\cup U_{C})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left[u\in U_{N}\right]+2\mathbb{P}\left[u\in U_{C}\setminus U_{N}\right]+(n-2)\mathbb{P}\left[w\in N^{*}\cap U_{N}\right]

(71)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(n-2)\mathbb{P}\left[w\in N^{*}\cap(U_{C}\setminus U_{N})\right].

We bound each of these four terms separately.

To begin, we note that, for any vertex
x\in V(G)
, we have

B_{G^{+}}(x,k)\subseteq B_{G}(x,k)\cup B_{G}(u,k)\cup B_{G}(v,k)

and so by
Proposition

D.1
for any
s\geq 3(7c)^{k}
,

(72)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(x,k)|\geq s\right]\leq 3\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(x,k)|\geq\frac{s}{3}\right]\leq 3k\exp\left(-\left(\frac{s}{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{k}}\right).

In particular,

(73)

2\mathbb{P}\left[u\in U_{N}\right]=2\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(u,k)|>t_{k}\right]\leq 6k\exp\left(-\left(\frac{t_{k}}{3}\right)^{\frac{1}{k}}\right)\leq 6k\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right),

where the last inequality follows from the definition of
t_{k}
in
5
. This bounds the first term of (
71
).

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[u\in U_{C}\setminus U_{N}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(u,k)|\leq t_{k}\text{ and }G^{+}[B_{G^{+}}(u,k)]\text{ contains a cycle}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(u,k)|\leq t_{k}\text{ and }|E(G^{+}[B_{G^{+}}(u)],k)|\geq|B_{G^{+}}(u,k)|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(u,k)|\leq t_{k}\text{ and }|E(G[B_{G^{+}}(u)],k)|\geq|B_{G^{+}}(u,k)|-1\right]

(74)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=3}^{t_{k}}\binom{n-2}{s-2}2^{s^{2}}p^{s-1}=O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

where we have used that
v\in B_{G^{+}}(u,k)
since
uv\in E(G^{+})
, bounding the second term of (
71
).

To bound the third term in
71
, we note, recalling that
N^{*}\coloneqq B_{G}(u,k)\cup B_{G}(v,k)
, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[w\in N^{*}\cap U_{N}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)|>t_{k}\text{ and }w\in N^{*}\right]

(75)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|>\frac{t_{k}}{2}\text{ and }w\in N^{*}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[|N^{*}|>\frac{t_{k}}{2}\text{ and }w\in N^{*}\right].

For the first term in (
75
), we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|>\frac{t_{k}}{2}\text{ and }w\in N^{*}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|>\frac{t_{k}}{2}\text{ and }B_{G}(w,k)\cap\{u,v\}\neq\varnothing\right]

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|=s\right]\mathbb{P}\left[B_{G}(w,k)\cap\{u,v\}\neq\varnothing\;\middle|\;|B_{G}(w,k)|=s\right].

However, conditional on
|B_{G}(w,k)|=s
, we have that
B_{G}(w,k)\setminus\{w\}
is uniformly distributed in
\binom{V(G)\setminus\{w\}}{s-1}
. Hence, by (
72
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|>\frac{t_{k}}{2}\text{ and }w\in N^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|=s\right]\frac{2\binom{n-2}{s-2}}{\binom{n-1}{s-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(w,k)|\geq s\right]\frac{2(s-1)}{n-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n-2}\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{\infty}ks\exp\left(-s^{\frac{1}{k}}\right)

(76)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2k}{n-2}\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right),

where the last inequality follows by
Proposition

D.2
with
a=m_{1}=1
and
m_{2}=k
, since
t_{k}\coloneqq(10ck)^{2k}\geq\max\{(4(k+1))^{2k},1\}
.

A similar calculation, noting that conditional on
|N^{*}|=s
, we have that
N^{*}\setminus\{u,v\}
is uniformly distributed in
\binom{V(G)\setminus\{u,v\}}{s-2}
, leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|N^{*}|>\frac{t_{k}}{2}\text{ and }w\in N^{*}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left[|N^{*}|=s\right]\mathbb{P}\left[w\in N^{*}\;\middle|\;|N^{*}|=s\right].

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left[|N^{*}|\geq s\right]\frac{\binom{n-3}{s-3}}{\binom{n-2}{s-2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{n}2\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G}(u,k)|\geq\frac{s}{2}\right]\frac{s-2}{n-2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2}{n-2}\sum_{s=\frac{t_{k}}{2}+1}^{n}ks\exp\left(-\left(\frac{s}{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{k}}\right)

(77)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2k}{n-2}\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right),

where the last inequality follows from
Proposition

D.2
with
a=2,m_{1}=1
and
m_{2}=k
, since
t_{k}\coloneqq(10ck)^{2k}\geq\max\{2(4(k+1))^{2k},2^{k+1}\}
.

Hence, we can bound the third term in
71
, using
75
,
76
, and
77
, as

(78)

\displaystyle(n-2)\mathbb{P}\left[w\in N^{*}\cap U_{N}\right]\leq 4k\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right).

Finally, we bound the last term in
71
.
Suppose that
w\in N^{*}\cap(U_{C}\setminus U_{N})
. Since
w\in N^{*}
,
|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)\cap\{u,v\}|\neq\varnothing
and since
w\in U_{C}
, we have that
G^{+}[B_{G^{+}}(w,k)]
contains a cycle.

If
|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)\cap\{u,v\}|=1
, then
G^{+}[B_{G^{+}}(w,k)]=G[B_{G}(w,k)]
is connected and has a cycle, thus
|E(G[B_{G^{+}}(w)],k)|\geq|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)|
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}
be the event that
|E(G[B_{G^{+}}(w)],k)|\geq|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)|
.
If instead
|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)\cap\{u,v\}|=2
, then
G^{+}[B_{G^{+}}(w,k)]
is connected and has a cycle. Thus,
|E(G^{+}[B_{G^{+}}(w)],k)|\geq|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)]|
which implies that
|E(G[B_{G^{+}}(w)],k)|\geq|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)|-1
. Let
\mathcal{E}_{2}
be the event that
|E(G[B_{G^{+}}(w)],k)|\geq|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)|-1
.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[w\in N^{*}\cap(U_{C}\setminus U_{N})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)\cap\{u,v\}|=1\text{ and }|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)|\leq t_{k}\text{ and }\mathcal{E}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{P}\left[|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)\cap\{u,v\}|=2\text{ and }|B_{G^{+}}(w,k)|\leq t_{k}\text{ and }\mathcal{E}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=3}^{t_{k}}2\binom{n}{s-2}2^{s^{2}}p^{s}+\sum_{s=3}^{t_{k}}\binom{n}{s-3}2^{s^{2}}p^{s-1}

(79)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right).

Finally, by
71
,
73
,
74
,
78
, and
79
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|N^{*}\cap(U_{N}\cup U_{C})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 6k\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)+4k\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right)+(n-2)\cdot O\left(\frac{1}{n^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq 10k\exp\left(-2c^{2}k^{2}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

establishing
(ii)
.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of Lemma
6.3

All asymptotics in this proof are as
N\rightarrow\infty
.
Let
Y
be the number of bins with at most
1
ball and note that
Y=N-Z
and so
\mathbb{V}\left[Y\right]=\mathbb{V}\left[Z\right]
.
For
i\in[N]
, let

I_{i}\coloneqq\mathds{1}_{\{\text{Bin $i$ has at most $1$ ball}\}},\quad\text{ so that }\quad Y=\sum_{i\in[N]}I_{i}.

Hence

(80)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left[Z\right]=\mathbb{V}\left[Y\right]=\sum_{i\in[N]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]^{2}\right)+\sum_{i\neq j}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}I_{j}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[I_{j}\right]\right).

Note that, for each
i\in[N]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{m}+\frac{m}{N}\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{m-1}=\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{m}{N}\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{-1}\right)

(81)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{m}{N}\left(1+\frac{1}{N}\right)\right)+O(N^{-2}).

Let
i,j\in[N]
with
i\neq j
. We have

(82)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[I_{j}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]^{2}=\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{2m}\left(1+\frac{2m}{N}\left(1+\frac{1}{N}\right)+\frac{m^{2}}{N^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2}{N}\right)\right)+O(N^{-2}).

Using that

\frac{\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{2m}}{\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}}=\left(1+\frac{\frac{1}{N^{2}}}{1-\frac{2}{N}}\right)^{m}=\left(1+\frac{1}{N(N-2)}\right)^{m}=1+\frac{m}{N^{2}}+O(N^{-2}),

we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[I_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{m}{N^{2}}\right)\left(1+\frac{2m}{N}\left(1+\frac{1}{N}\right)+\frac{m^{2}}{N^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2}{N}\right)\right)+O(N^{-2})

(83)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{2m}{N}+\frac{3m}{N^{2}}+\frac{m^{2}}{N^{2}}+\frac{4m^{2}}{N^{3}}+\frac{m^{3}}{N^{4}}\right)+O(N^{-2}).

Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}I_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}+\frac{2m}{N}\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m-1}+\frac{m(m-1)}{N^{2}}\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m-2}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{2m}{N}\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{-1}+\frac{m^{2}}{N^{2}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m}\right)\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{2m}{N}\left(1+\frac{2}{N}\right)+\frac{m^{2}}{N^{2}}\left(1-\frac{1}{m}\right)\left(1+\frac{4}{N}\right)\right)+O(N^{-2})

(84)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{2m}{N}+\frac{3m}{N^{2}}+\frac{m^{2}}{N^{2}}+\frac{4m^{2}}{N^{3}}\right)+O(N^{-2}).

From
84
and
83
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}I_{j}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[I_{i}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[I_{j}\right]=-\frac{m^{3}}{N^{4}}\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}+O(N^{-2}).

Together with
80
,
81
, and
82
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left[Z\right]

\displaystyle=N\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{m}\left(1+\frac{m}{N}\right)-N\left(1-\frac{1}{N}\right)^{2m}\left(1+\frac{2m}{N}+\frac{m^{2}}{N^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-(N-1)\frac{m^{3}}{N^{3}}\left(1-\frac{2}{N}\right)^{m}+O(1)

\displaystyle=h\left(\frac{m}{N}\right)N+O(1).

Finally
\mathbb{V}\left[Z\right]\geq\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{m}{N}\right)^{2}N
, follows since
h(x)>\frac{1}{3}x^{2}
for
0<x<\frac{1}{100}
(proof omitted).

Appendix F
Proof of Lemma
7.2

As in
50
, we can write
\widehat{M}_{k}=\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}Y_{U}
, where
\mathcal{U}\coloneqq\{U\subseteq V(G)\colon 1\leq|U|\leq t_{k}\}
,

Y_{U}\coloneqq\sum_{v\in U}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}\\
v(T)=|U|\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T,r)}I(U,v,T,r),

and
I(U,v,T,r)\coloneqq\mathds{1}_{\{B_{G}(v,k)=U\}}\mathds{1}_{\{(G[U],v)\cong(T,r)\}}
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left[\widehat{M}_{k}\right]=

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\mathbb{V}\left[Y_{U}\right]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\\
U_{1}\neq U_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\mathbb{V}\left[Y_{U}\right]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\\
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\\
|U_{1}\cap U_{2}|=1\end{subarray}}\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})

(85)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=2}^{t_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{i,\dots,t_{k}\}\\
(j_{1},j_{2})\neq(i,i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\\
|U_{1}|=j_{1},|U_{2}|=j_{2}\\
|U_{1}\cap U_{2}|=i\end{subarray}}\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}}).

We will show that each of these four sums is linear in
n
.

Let us first fix some
1\leq j\leq t_{k}
and
U\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
with
|U|=j
and estimate
\mathbb{V}\left[Y_{U}\right]
. Note that, since
p=\frac{c}{n}
, we have for any constants
a_{1},a_{2}>0
, that

(1-p)^{a_{1}n+a_{2}}=e^{-a_{1}c}+O(p).

For
(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}
, we denote by
\mathrm{aut}(T,r)
the number of automorphisms of
T
that map
r
to itself. Let
\mathcal{T}_{j}\coloneqq\{(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}\colon v(T)=j\}
. We can calculate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in U}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}\\
v(T)=|U|\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T,r)}\mathbb{P}\left[B_{G}(v,k)=U\text{ and }(G[U],v)\cong(T,r)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}_{j}}\frac{\beta_{(T,r)}j!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r)}p^{j-1}(1-p)^{(n-j)|B_{T}(r,k-1)|+\binom{j}{2}-j+1}

(86)

\displaystyle=O(p^{j-1}),

where we used that, for
(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}_{j}
,

\left|\left\{E\subseteq\binom{[j]}{2}\colon(([j],E),1)\cong(T,r)\right\}\right|=\frac{(j-1)!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r)}.

On the other hand we can write

\displaystyle Y_{U}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{1}\in U\\
v_{2}\in U\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j}\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}I(U,v_{1},T_{1},r_{2})I(U,v_{2},T_{2},r_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in U}\sum_{(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}_{j}}\beta_{(T,r)}^{2}I(U,v,T,r)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{1},v_{2}\in U\\
v_{2}\neq v_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T,r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j}\\
r_{2}\in V(T)\\
r_{2}\neq r_{1}\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T,r_{1})}\beta_{(T,r_{2})}I(U,v_{1},T_{1},r_{2})I(U,v_{2},T_{2},r_{2}).

Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}_{j}}\frac{\beta_{(T,r)}^{2}j!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r)}p^{j-1}(1-p)^{(n-j)|B_{T}(r,k-1)|+\binom{j}{2}-j+1}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{(T,r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{2}\in V(T)\\
r_{2}\neq r_{1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T,r_{1})}\beta_{(T,r_{2})}j!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r_{1},r_{2})}p^{j-1}(1-p)^{(n-j)|B_{T}(r_{1},k-1)\cup B_{T}(r_{2},k-1)|+\binom{j}{2}-j+1}

\displaystyle=p^{j-1}\sum_{(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}_{j}}\left(\frac{\beta_{(T,r)}^{2}j!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r)}e^{-c|B_{T}(r,k-1)|}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r^{\prime}\in V(T)\\
r^{\prime}\neq r\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T,r)}\beta_{(T,r^{\prime})}j!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r,r^{\prime})}e^{-c|B_{T}(r,k-1)\cup B_{T}(r^{\prime},k-1)|}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+O(p^{j})

(87)

\displaystyle=p^{j-1}K^{(1)}_{j}(k,c)+O(p^{j}),

where
\mathrm{aut}(T,r,r^{\prime})
is the number of automorphisms that map
r
to
r
and
r^{\prime}
to
r^{\prime}
and we have set

\displaystyle K^{(1)}_{j}(k,c)\coloneqq\sum_{(T,r)\in\mathcal{T}_{j}}\left(\frac{\beta_{(T,r)}^{2}j!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r)}e^{-c|B_{T}(r,k-1)|}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r^{\prime}\in V(T)\\
r^{\prime}\neq r\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T,r)}\beta_{(T,r^{\prime})}j!}{\mathrm{aut}(T,r,r^{\prime})}e^{-c|B_{T}(r,k-1)\cup B_{T}(r^{\prime},k-1)|}\right).

Hence, by (
86
) and (
87
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left[Y_{U}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U}^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U}\right]^{2}=p^{j-1}K^{(1)}_{j}(k,c)+O(p^{j})+O(p^{2j-2})=p^{j-1}K^{(1)}_{j}(k,c)+O(p^{j}).

Hence,

\displaystyle\sum_{U\in\mathcal{U}_{k}}\mathbb{V}\left[Y_{U}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{t_{k}}\binom{n}{j}\left(p^{j-1}K^{(1)}_{j}(k,c)+O(p^{j})\right)

\displaystyle=n\sum_{j=2}^{t_{k}}\frac{c^{j-1}K^{(1)}_{j}(k,c)}{j!}+O(1)

(88)

\displaystyle=nK^{(1)}(k,c)+O(1),

where we have set

\displaystyle K^{(1)}(k,c)\coloneqq\sum_{j=2}^{t_{k}}\frac{c^{j-1}K^{(1)}_{j}(k,c)}{j!}.

Next, let us fix some
1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}
and
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
with
|U_{1}|=j_{1}
,
|U_{2}|=j_{2}
, and
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing
and estimate
\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})
.
We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{1}\in U_{1}\\
v_{2}\in U_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in T_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left(\mathbb{E}\left[I(U_{1},v_{1},T_{1},r_{1})I(U_{2},v_{2},T_{2},r_{2})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[I(U_{1},v_{1},T_{1},r_{1})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[I(U_{2},v_{2},T_{2},r_{2})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in T_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}{\mathrm{aut}(T_{1},r_{1})\mathrm{aut}(T_{2},r_{2})}\cdot p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2}(1-p)^{(n-j_{1})|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|+\binom{j_{1}}{2}-j_{1}+1}\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\cdot(1-p)^{(n-j_{2})|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|+\binom{j_{2}}{2}-j_{2}+1}\left((1-p)^{-|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|\cdot|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|}-1\right).

Since

(1-p)^{-|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|\cdot|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|}-1=|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|\cdot|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|\cdot p+O(p^{2}),

we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})

\displaystyle=p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in T_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}{\mathrm{aut}(T_{1},r_{1})\mathrm{aut}(T_{2},r_{2})}|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|\cdot|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot e^{-c(|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|+|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|)}+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}})

\displaystyle=p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}K^{(2)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}}),

where we have set

\displaystyle K^{(2)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in T_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}{\mathrm{aut}(T_{1},r_{1})\mathrm{aut}(T_{2},r_{2})}|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|\cdot|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot e^{-c(|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|+|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|)}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\\
U_{1}\cap U_{2}=\varnothing\end{subarray}}\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}}\binom{n}{j_{1}}\binom{n-j_{1}}{j_{2}}\left(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}K^{(2)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}})\right)

\displaystyle=n\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}}\frac{c^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}K^{(2)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)}{j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}+O(1)

(89)

\displaystyle=nK^{(2)}(k,c)+O(1),

where we have set

\displaystyle K^{(2)}(k,c)\coloneqq\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}}\frac{c^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}K^{(2)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)}{j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}.

Next, let us fix some
1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}
and
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}
with
|U_{1}|=j_{1}
,
|U_{2}|=j_{2}
,
|U_{1}\cap U_{2}|=1
, and
U_{1}\neq U_{2}
and estimate
\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})
.
Let
(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}
and
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{2}}
and suppose that
U_{1}=B_{G}(v_{1},k)
,
(U_{1},v_{1})\cong(T_{1},r_{1})
,
U_{2}=B_{G}(v_{2},k)
, and
(U_{2},v_{2})\cong(T_{2},r_{2})
.
Suppose first that
|U_{1}|=1
. Then since
G[U_{2}]
is connected we have that
B_{G}(v_{1},k)\neq U_{1}
, a contradiction. Hence we may assume
|U_{1}|\geq 2
and similarly
|U_{2}|\geq 2
.
Let
x
be the vertex in
U_{1}\cap U_{2}
. Note that since
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
are connected,
x
is adjacent to a vertex in both
U_{1}\setminus U_{2}
and
U_{2}\setminus U_{1}
. In particular, since
U_{1}=B_{G}(v_{1},k)
and
U_{2}=B_{G}(v_{2},k)
, it follows that
x\in\partial_{G}(v_{1},k)\cap\partial_{G}(v_{2},k)
.
Hence, by a similar calculation as before, noting that any automorphism of
T
which maps
r
to itself also maps
\partial_{T}(r,k)
to itself,

\displaystyle\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}{\mathrm{aut}(T_{1},r_{1})\mathrm{aut}(T_{2},r_{2})}\left(\frac{|\partial_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k)|\cdot|\partial_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k)|}{j_{1}j_{2}}(1-p)^{-|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|\cdot|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|}-1\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2}(1-p)^{(n-j_{1})|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|+\binom{j_{1}}{2}-j_{1}+1}(1-p)^{(n-j_{2})|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|+\binom{j_{2}}{2}-j_{2}+1}

\displaystyle=p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}{\mathrm{aut}(T_{1},r_{1})\mathrm{aut}(T_{2},r_{2})}\left(\frac{|\partial_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k)|\cdot|\partial_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k)|}{j_{1}j_{2}}-1\right)\cdot

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot e^{-c(|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|+|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|)}+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1})

\displaystyle=p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2}K^{(3)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1}),

where we have set

\displaystyle K^{(3)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)\coloneqq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\frac{\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}{\mathrm{aut}(T_{1},r_{1})\mathrm{aut}(T_{2},r_{2})}\left(\frac{|\partial_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k)|\cdot|\partial_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k)|}{j_{1}j_{2}}-1\right)\cdot e^{-c(|B_{T_{1}}(r_{1},k-1)|+|B_{T_{2}}(r_{2},k-1)|)}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\\
|U_{1}\cap U_{2}|=1\end{subarray}}\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}}\binom{n}{j_{1}}j_{1}\binom{n-j_{1}}{j_{2}}\left(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2}K^{(3)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-1})\right)

\displaystyle=n\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}}\frac{c^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2}j_{1}K^{(3)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)}{j_{1}!\,(j_{2}-1)!}+O(1)

(90)

\displaystyle=nK^{(3)}(k,c)+O(1),

where we have set

\displaystyle K^{(3)}(k,c)\coloneqq\sum_{1\leq j_{1},j_{2}\leq t_{k}}\frac{c^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2}j_{1}K^{(3)}_{j_{1},j_{2}}(k,c)}{j_{1}!\,(j_{2}-1)!}.

Finally, let us fix some
j_{1},j_{2}\in[t_{k}]
and
2\leq i\leq\min\{j_{1},j_{2}\}
. Let
V_{1}\coloneqq[j_{1}]

and
V_{2}\coloneqq\{j_{1}-i+1,\dots,j_{1}+j_{2}-i\}
. For
(T_{1},r_{1}),(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}
with
v(T_{1})=j_{1}
and
v(T_{2})=j_{2}
, we let
\mathcal{F}_{i}(T_{1},r_{1},T_{2},r_{2})
be the set of tuples
(E_{1},v_{1},E_{2},v_{2})
with
E_{1}\subseteq\binom{V_{1}}{2}
,
v_{1}\in V_{1}
,
E_{2}\subseteq\binom{V_{2}}{2}
, and
v_{2}\in V_{2}
such that following hold with
H_{1}=(V_{1},E_{1})
and
H_{2}=(V_{2},E_{2})
.

(i)

(H_{1},v_{1})\cong(T_{1},r_{1})
and
(H_{2},v_{2})\cong(T_{2},r_{2})
,

(ii)

E_{1}\cap\binom{V_{1}\cap V_{2}}{2}=E_{2}\cap\binom{V_{1}\cap V_{2}}{2}=E_{1}\cap E_{2}
,

(iii)

for all
w\in B_{H_{1}}(v_{1},k-1)\cap V_{1}\cap V_{2}
with
N_{H_{1}}(w)\setminus V_{2}\neq\varnothing
, we have
w\in\partial_{H_{2}}(v_{2},k)
,

(iv)

for all
w\in B_{H_{2}}(v_{2},k-1)\cap V_{1}\cap V_{2}
with
N_{H_{2}}(w)\setminus V_{1}\neq\varnothing
, we have
w\in\partial_{H_{1}}(v_{1},k)
, and

(v)

v_{1}\neq v_{2}
.

Let
U_{1},U_{2}\subseteq V(G)
with
U_{1}\neq U_{2}
,
|U_{1}|=j_{1}
,
|U_{2}|=j_{2}
, and
|U_{1}\cap U_{2}|=i
. Without loss of generality assume that
U_{1}=V_{1}
and
U_{2}=V_{2}
. Now note that, for
(T_{1},r_{1}),(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}
,
v_{1}\in U_{1}
, and
v_{2}\in U_{2}
, we have that if

U_{i}=B_{G}(v_{i},k)\text{ and }(G[U_{i}],v_{i})\cong(T_{i},r_{i})\quad\text{ for }i=1,2,

then
(E(G[U_{1}]),v_{1},E(G[U_{2}]),v_{2})\in\mathcal{F}_{i}(T_{1},r_{1},T_{2},r_{2})
.
Furthermore, if
E_{i}=E(G[B_{G}(v_{i},k)])
for
i\in\{1,2\}
, then the converse also holds. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}\right]=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}\cdot

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(E_{1},v_{1},E_{2},v_{2})\\
\in\mathcal{F}_{i}(T_{1},r_{1},T_{2},r_{2})\end{subarray}}p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2-|E_{1}\cap E_{2}|}(1-p)^{\binom{j_{1}+j_{2}-i}{2}-|E_{1}\cup E_{2}|+|B_{H_{1}}(v_{1},k-1)\cup B_{H_{2}}(v_{2},k-1)|(n-j_{1}-j_{2}+i)}.

It is easy to verify that the above sum is dominated by the contribution from the set of tuples
(E_{1},v_{1},E_{2},v_{2})\in\mathcal{F}_{i}(T_{1},r_{1},T_{2},r_{2})
with
|E_{1}\cap E_{2}|=i-1
, which we denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{i}(T_{1},r_{1},T_{2},r_{2})
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}Y_{U_{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(E_{1},v_{1},E_{2},v_{2})\\
\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{i}(T_{1},r_{1},T_{2},r_{2})\end{subarray}}e^{-c|B_{H_{1}}(v_{1},k-1)\cup B_{H_{2}}(v_{2},k-1)|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i})

\displaystyle=p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i-1}K^{(4)}_{j_{1},j_{2},i}(k,c)+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i}),

where we have set

\displaystyle K^{(4)}_{j_{1},j_{2},i}(k,c)\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(T_{1},r_{1})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{1}}\\
(T_{2},r_{2})\in\mathcal{T}_{j_{2}}\end{subarray}}\beta_{(T_{1},r_{1})}\beta_{(T_{2},r_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(E_{1},v_{1},E_{2},v_{2})\\
\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{i}(T_{1},r_{1},T_{2},r_{2})\end{subarray}}e^{-c|B_{H_{1}}(v_{1},k-1)\cup B_{H_{2}}(v_{2},k-1)|}.

Since
i\geq 2
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{1}}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{U_{2}}\right]=O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-2})=O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i})
.
Hence,
\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})=O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i})+p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i-1}K^{(4)}_{j_{1},j_{2},i}(k,c)
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{t_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{i,\dots,t_{k}\}\\
(j_{1},j_{2})\neq(i,i)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{U}_{k}\\
|U_{1}|=j_{1},|U_{2}|=j_{2}\\
|U_{1}\cap U_{2}|=i\end{subarray}}\mathrm{cov}(Y_{U_{1}},Y_{U_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{t_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{i,\dots,t_{k}\}\\
(j_{1},j_{2})\neq(i,i)\end{subarray}}\binom{n}{j_{1}}\binom{j_{1}}{i}\binom{n-i}{j_{2}}\left(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i-1}K^{(4)}_{j_{1},j_{2},i}(k,c)+O(p^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{t_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{i,\dots,t_{k}\}\\
(j_{1},j_{2})\neq(i,i)\end{subarray}}n\cdot\frac{c^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i-1}\binom{j_{1}}{i}}{j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}K^{(4)}_{j_{1},j_{2},i}(k,c)+O(1)

(91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K^{(4)}(k,c)n+O(1),

where we have set

\displaystyle K^{(4)}(k,c)\coloneqq\sum_{i=2}^{t_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j_{1},j_{2}\in\{i,\dots,t_{k}\}\\
(j_{1},j_{2})\neq(i,i)\end{subarray}}\frac{c^{j_{1}+j_{2}-i-1}\binom{j_{1}}{i}}{j_{1}!\,j_{2}!}K^{(4)}_{j_{1},j_{2},i}(k,c).

It now follows from
85
,
88
,
89
,
90
, and
91
, that

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}\left[\widehat{M}_{k}\right]=(K^{(1)}(k,c)+K^{(2)}(k,c)+K^{(3)}(k,c)+K^{(4)}(k,c))n+O(1).

Hence, the statement of the lemma is satisfied with

K(k,c,\bm{\beta})\coloneqq K^{(1)}(k,c)+K^{(2)}(k,c)+K^{(3)}(k,c)+K^{(4)}(k,c).
